# Source and test mapping

One row per ported file (spec §5, §8.1). `aeron:` paths are relative to the Aeron `1.53.3` checkout and `agrona:`
paths to the Agrona `2.6.1` checkout (`npm run fetch:refs`). `npm run check:mapping` checks the **Tests** and
**Skipped tests** tables against both sides: every Java test name has a TS test of the same name and the reverse,
the counts are current, and every `it.skip` is listed with its reason.

## Sources

| Java source | TypeScript source | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/BitUtil.java` | `src/agrona/BitUtil.ts` | `int`/`long` overloads: the `long` ones are `findNextPositivePowerOfTwoLong`, `alignLong`, `isEvenLong`, `isPowerOfTwoLong`; they and `isAligned` throw `RangeError` for an argument or a result that is not a safe integer (spec D5). `byte[]` is `Uint8Array`; `toHexByteArray` takes a `Uint8Array` or a `string` (the `CharSequence` overload) and throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` / `StringIndexOutOfBoundsException` / `NegativeArraySizeException` where Java does. `fromHexByteArray` throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` for a byte above 127 through a private `fromHexDigit`, as the Java table lookup does. `next` and `previous` wrap like an `int`. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/Strings.java` | `src/agrona/Strings.ts` | `isEmpty` is a type guard. `parseIntOrDefault` uses `Integer.parseInt` from `jdk.ts`. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/LangUtil.java` | `src/agrona/LangUtil.ts` | `rethrowUnchecked` returns `never`. There is no checked-exception erasure to do, so the private generic `rethrow` is a plain `throw`. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/AsciiEncoding.java` | `src/agrona/AsciiEncoding.ts` | `CharSequence` is `string` (`charAt` and `subSequence` throw `StringIndexOutOfBoundsException` where Java's do), `char` and `byte` are `number`. `long` results are `number` (spec D5): `parseLongAscii` accumulates in a number and switches to `bigint` only when the value leaves +/-(2^53 - 1), then throws `LongOverflowError` carrying the exact long Java returns. `digitCountLong` of a `number` is exact-double arithmetic over `LONG_POW_10`; of a `bigint` it uses `LONG_DIGITS` (a `BigInt64Array`). The SWAR helpers that take eight bytes at once keep their `bigint` form (`isEightDigitAsciiEncodedNumber`, `parseEightDigitsLittleEndian`); the parsers use the added `...Words(high, low)` forms over two int32 words, with the carry and borrow of the 64-bit add and subtract done by hand. `LONG_POW_10` is a `number[]` (every power of ten up to 10^18 is exact); `*_VALUE_DIGITS` and `MIN/MAX_*_VALUE` are `Int32Array` / `Uint8Array`. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/AsciiNumberFormatException.java` | `src/agrona/AsciiNumberFormatException.ts` | Extends `NumberFormatException` from `jdk.ts`. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/SystemUtil.java` | `src/agrona/SystemUtil.ts` | Only the pure parts: `parseSize`, `formatSize`, `parseDuration`, `formatDuration` and what they use, plus the package-private `isX64Arch(String)`. The digits are parsed to a `bigint` and checked against Java's 2^63 - 1 bounds, so the overflow errors are Java's; a result outside +/-(2^53 - 1) throws `LongOverflowError` (`parseSize`) or, for `parseDuration`, saturates at 2^53 - 1 (a bare number, `ns`, `us`, `ms` and `s` alike), the one saturating function of spec D5. `getSizeAsInt`, `getSizeAsLong` and `getDurationInNanos` (T7b) read a system property of `System` (below) and parse it with `parseSize` / `parseDuration`; `getSizeAsInt` rejects a value above `Integer.MAX_VALUE` with Agrona's message. Addition, not in Agrona: `isDebuggerAttached()` is the `node:inspector` being active (Java looks for the JDWP agent), used by `CommonContext.checkDebugTimeout`. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/ErrorHandler.java` | `src/agrona/ErrorHandler.ts` | Interface; `Throwable` is `Error`. |
| (no Java file) | `src/agrona/jdk.ts` | JDK stand-ins that Agrona and its tests use (spec D10): `ByteOrder`, the exception classes (`Exception`, `RuntimeException`, `IllegalArgumentException`, `IllegalStateException`, `NumberFormatException`, `ArithmeticException`, `NullPointerException`, `UnsupportedOperationException`, `IndexOutOfBoundsException`, `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`, `StringIndexOutOfBoundsException`, `NegativeArraySizeException`, each with `name` set to its class name), `LongOverflowError` (a `RangeError`), `Character.isDigit` (a table built once), `Integer` (`parseInt`, like the builder's private `parseLong`, accepts ASCII digits only, where Java accepts any Unicode digit), `Long` (`MAX_VALUE` / `MIN_VALUE` are the +/-(2^53 - 1) bounds), `Byte`, `Short`, `TimeUnit.toNanos` (from `NANOSECONDS` to `DAYS`), `ThreadLocalRandom`, `Objects.checkFromIndexSize`, `Appendable`, `newStringAscii` (`new String(bytes, US_ASCII)`), `longToNumber`, `longValueExact`, and `dataViewGetLong` / `dataViewSetLong` (a `long` in a `DataView` as a `number`, without allocating). `Long.checkInt64` (a `bigint` `long`: `RangeError` outside [-2^63, 2^63 - 1], spec D5). T7b adds: `systemProperties` (the one `Map<string, string>` behind `System`, here and not in `jdkLang.ts` so that `Integer.getInteger`, `Long.getLong` and `Boolean.getBoolean` need no import of the file that imports this one), `Integer.getInteger(name, def)` and `Long.getLong(name, def)` (the property decoded with `Integer.decode` / `Long.decode` semantics: sign, `0x` / `0X` / `#` hex, a leading `0` octal; a malformed or out of range value gives `def`; a `Long.getLong` value inside Java's `long` but outside +/-(2^53 - 1) is a `LongOverflowError`), `Boolean` (`getBoolean`: the property equals "true" ignoring case), `multiplyExact(a, b)` (`Math.multiplyExact(long, long)`: the product as a `bigint`, outside Java's `long` an `ArithmeticException("long overflow")`, inside it but outside +/-(2^53 - 1) a `LongOverflowError`), `ClassNotFoundException`, `ThreadLocalRandom.nextBoolean`, and `TimeUnit.toMillis` and `convert`. |

| (no Java file) | `test/support/Assertions.ts`, `test/support/Mockito.ts`, `test/support/ByteBuffer.ts` | Test helpers: JUnit's `assertThrows` / `assertThrowsExactly` (which return the exception) and `assertEquals` / `assertNotEquals` for objects, the part of Mockito's `mock` that the tests use, and a small `java.nio.ByteBuffer` stand-in. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/DirectBuffer.java` | `src/agrona/DirectBuffer.ts` | Interface, plus a `const DirectBuffer` of the same name with the interface constants (`STR_HEADER_LEN`, `DISABLE_BOUNDS_CHECKS_PROP_NAME`, `SHOULD_BOUNDS_CHECK`, always `true`: there are no system properties). `hashCode` and `equals`, which every Java object has, are declared. `long` is `number` (`getLong` throws `RangeError` outside +/-(2^53 - 1)), `getLongBigInt` reads 64 bits. Overloads that differ by parameter type (`getStringAscii`, `getBytes`, `wrap`) are TypeScript overload signatures over one implementation. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/MutableDirectBuffer.java` | `src/agrona/MutableDirectBuffer.ts` | Interface. `putLong` throws `RangeError`, writing nothing, for a value that is not a safe integer; `putLongBigInt` writes 64 bits. The `String` and `CharSequence` overloads of `putStringAscii` and `putStringWithoutLengthAscii` are one method over `string \| null`. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/concurrent/AtomicBuffer.java` | `src/agrona/concurrent/AtomicBuffer.ts` | Interface, plus `const AtomicBuffer` with `ALIGNMENT`. Over an `ArrayBuffer`, the volatile, ordered, release, acquire, opaque and atomic operations are plain single-thread operations with the same names and results; over a `SharedArrayBuffer` they use `Atomics` (see `UnsafeBuffer`, spec D16). Over a `SharedArrayBuffer` the `long` ones are 64-bit atomics through `LongStripes`. Port-only additions (spec D16), declared after `putByteVolatile`: `getLongVolatileHalves(index, out)`, `putLongVolatileHalves(index, lo, hi)`, `compareAndSetLongHalves(index, expectedLo, expectedHi, updateLo, updateHi)` and `getAndAddLongHalves(index, delta, out)`, the full 64-bit range as an `int` pair for the raw tails (D12). |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/AbstractMutableDirectBuffer.java` | `src/agrona/AbstractMutableDirectBuffer.ts` | `sun.misc.Unsafe` reads and writes of `byteArray` at `addressOffset + index` are `DataView` calls on `_dataView` at `_addressOffset + index`. Fields are `_byteArray`, `_dataView`, `_addressOffset`, `_capacity` (the Java names clash with the accessors). Byte order is the `DataView` `littleEndian` flag instead of `reverseBytes`. The long parsers accumulate in a number and switch to `bigint` only when the value leaves +/-(2^53 - 1), then throw `LongOverflowError`; the 8-digit checks use two int32 words. `setMemory` is `Uint8Array.fill`. Copies allocate nothing and move bytes as memmove does for overlapping views of one `ArrayBuffer` (`copyWithin`, or a loop in the safe direction). UTF-8 uses `TextEncoder` / `TextDecoder` (a lone surrogate is encoded as '?', as `String.getBytes(UTF_8)` does; a byte order mark is kept). |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/concurrent/UnsafeBuffer.java` | `src/agrona/concurrent/UnsafeBuffer.ts` | Constructors and `wrap` take a `Uint8Array` (`byte[]`), an `ArrayBuffer` (the `ByteBuffer`, as a `Uint8Array` over all of it, reused when the same `ArrayBuffer` is wrapped again) or a `DirectBuffer`, with an optional `(offset, length)`; the Java overloads differing only in arity are one method. `wrap(DirectBuffer)` shares the other `UnsafeBuffer`'s `DataView` and allocates one only for a foreign `DirectBuffer` or a new `Uint8Array`. `verifyAlignment` checks `byteOffset + addressOffset` of the window in its `ArrayBuffer` and reports that value as `addressOffset` (spec D9). `toString` is Java's text, with `byteBuffer=null`. **Shared backing (spec D16):** every place that takes an `ArrayBuffer` also takes a `SharedArrayBuffer` (and a `Uint8Array` over one). Whether the backing is shared is decided once, at `wrap`; a shared buffer keeps cached `Int32Array` / `Int16Array` views over the whole `SharedArrayBuffer` (one set per `SharedArrayBuffer`, so wrapping the same one again allocates nothing) and an absolute base `byteOffset + addressOffset`. Over a shared buffer the `byte`, `short`, `char` and `int` `*Volatile`, `*Acquire`, `*Ordered`, `*Release`, `*Opaque`, `compareAndSetInt`, `compareAndExchangeInt`, `getAndAddInt`, `getAndSetInt` and `add*` forms are `Atomics.load` / `store` / `compareExchange` / `add` / `exchange` on the element `(base + index) >>> 2` (`>>> 1` for short and char, the byte itself for byte); the `long` forms are true 64-bit atomics on Java's word (8 little-endian bytes at the index) through the striped lock table `LongStripes` (spec D16, T2b): a writer takes the stripe with a test-and-set (`Atomics.or`), reads the payload with plain reads, stores the HIGH half (only when it changed) and then the LOW half with `Atomics.store` and releases the stripe (plus two, or as it was when nothing was written), a reader retries while the stripe is odd or changed and never writes it, and `getAndAddLong` range checks the sum (D5) and writes nothing when it is outside. They allocate nothing; each is one compact inlined body behind a tiny dispatcher on the backing. After 2^28 spins on a held stripe (a writer died holding it: a worker must never be `terminate()`d inside a buffer operation) an operation throws `IllegalStateException`; the port-only static `setStripeSpinCapForTests(spins)` lowers that cap for the kill test. Wrapping a `SharedArrayBuffer` in a worker that has no `LongStripes` table throws `IllegalStateException`. Plain gets and puts (`getInt`, `putInt`, ...) stay `DataView` calls on a shared buffer. Bounds checks come first; then an atomic access at an address that is not a multiple of its size throws `IllegalArgumentException('Unaligned atomic access: index=<i> size=<n>')` (a deviation, D16: Agrona has no such check and x86 tolerates it). A `SharedArrayBuffer` of 4 GiB or more is refused at `wrap`. Binding a `SharedArrayBuffer` on a big-endian platform throws `IllegalStateException`, and a growable `SharedArrayBuffer` is refused with `IllegalArgumentException`. Additions (spec D16): `getLongVolatileHalves`, `putLongVolatileHalves`, `compareAndSetLongHalves`, `getAndAddLongHalves` (see `AtomicBuffer`). The `*Seq` methods of the rejected two-slot lock do not exist. |
| (no Java file) | `src/agrona/concurrent/LongStripes.ts` | Port-only (spec D16), no Java counterpart. The striped lock table behind the 64-bit atomics of `UnsafeBuffer` on a `SharedArrayBuffer`: 1024 stripes, one `int` per 64-byte line, one process-wide `SharedArrayBuffer` created by the main thread at module load and handed to every worker through `worker_threads` environment data (`ENVIRONMENT_KEY`). `stripe(address)` hashes an absolute byte address (a multiple of 8) to a multiple of `STRIPE_STRIDE`. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/BufferUtil.java` | `src/agrona/BufferUtil.ts` | `ARRAY_BASE_OFFSET` is 0 (a typed array has no object header). `allocateDirectAligned` returns an `ArrayBuffer`: the alignment is checked to be a power of 2, then ignored. Port-only: `allocateShared(length, alignment)` returns a `SharedArrayBuffer` after the same check (spec D16). |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/collections/Hashing.java` | `src/agrona/collections/Hashing.ts` | `hash(int)`, `hash(int, int)` are `hash(value, mask?)`. The `long` overloads are `hashLong(value, mask?)`, `evenHashLong` (a `long` is a `number`, spec D5) and `hash(Object, int)` is `hashObject`. `hashLong` does the 64-bit xor-shift-multiply over two int32 words (`Math.imul` and 16-bit limbs, the product in a module `Int32Array` so that nothing is boxed) and is checked against a `bigint` reference in `test/extra`. `DEFAULT_LOAD_FACTOR` is `Math.fround(0.65)`, a float. `compoundKey` throws `LongOverflowError` (exact long) when the compound is outside +/-(2^53 - 1). Additions (spec D14, not in Java): `hashLongHalves(hi, lo, mask?)` and `evenHashLongHalves(hi, lo, mask)` compute what `hash(long, int)` and `evenHash(long, int)` compute for `(hi << 32) | (lo & 0xFFFFFFFF)`, from the two int halves; `hashLong` and `hashLongHalves` share one module function (`hashHalves`), so `hashLong` is unchanged. `hashLongHalves` is checked against a `bigint` reference of Agrona's `hash(long)` in `test/extra`. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/collections/ArrayUtil.java` | `src/agrona/collections/ArrayUtil.ts` | `T[]` is a JS array; `remove(T[], int)` is `removeAt` (a `number` element and an index cannot be told apart); `remove(T[], T)` compares with `===`. `newArray` ignores the component type. | `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/collections/ArrayListUtil.java` | `src/agrona/collections/ArrayListUtil.ts` | `ArrayList<T>` is `T[]`. `fastUnorderedRemove(list, index)` and `(list, index, lastIndex)` are one method with an optional `lastIndex`; `fastUnorderedRemove(list, T)` is `fastUnorderedRemoveElement`. Out-of-range indexes throw `IndexOutOfBoundsException` with `ArrayList`'s message, and `lastIndex` is removed before `index` is checked, as in Java. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/collections/CollectionUtil.java` | `src/agrona/collections/CollectionUtil.ts` | Needed by the maps (`validateLoadFactor`), ported whole. A `Map` is anything with `get` / `put`, a `List` is an array. `validateLoadFactor` compares as a float and prints the float as `Float.toString` does (`floatToString` in `jdkUtil.ts`). |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/collections/NullReference.java` | `src/agrona/collections/NullReference.ts` | Needed by `Int2NullableObjectHashMap`. |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/collections/Int2ObjectHashMap.java` | `src/agrona/collections/Int2ObjectHashMap.ts` | `Map<Integer, V>` is not a TS type, so the boxed overloads are the `int` ones: `get(Object)` / `get(int)`, `containsKey`, `remove(Object)` / `remove(int)`, `remove(Object, Object)` / `remove(int, V)` (one `remove(key, value?)` over two signatures), `put`, `computeIfAbsent`, `computeIfPresent`, `compute`, `merge`, `replace` (1 or 2 values), `putAll(Map)` / `putAll(Int2ObjectHashMap)` (one method, the fast path for an `Int2ObjectHashMap`) and `forEach(BiConsumer)` (which still delegates to `forEachInt`; `intForEach` is kept). Keys are an `Int32Array`, values an `unknown[]` of null for empty. `float loadFactor` is `Math.fround` and `(int)(capacity * loadFactor)` is done in float. The nested classes are top-level exports `Int2ObjectHashMapKeySet`, `...ValueCollection`, `...EntrySet`, `...KeyIterator`, `...ValueIterator`, `...EntryIterator`, `...MapEntry`; the fields and package-private members they use are public with `@internal` and an underscore (`_keys`, `_values`, `_size`, `_shouldAvoidAllocation`, `_mapNullValue`, `_unmapNullValue`, `_getMapped`, `compactChain`, `reset`, `findNext`, `allocateDuplicateEntry`) because TS has no package-private. `mapNullValue` / `unmapNullValue` stay `protected` and overridable. A `V` result is `V \| null`; a `null` argument still throws `NullPointerException("value cannot be null")`. The views extend `AbstractCollection` / `AbstractSet` of `jdkUtil.ts`, so `for ... of` and `forEach` work; `for ... of` over an `entrySet()` yields the shared iterator when `shouldAvoidAllocation` is true, as Java's for-each does. `Map.keySet().remove(Object)` / `remove(int)`, `contains(Object)` / `contains(int)` are one method each. Every keyed method starts with `key |= 0`, Java's `(int)` cast at the call site, so that a key past 32 bits is the same key in every method. A function result of `undefined` is `null` (`?? null`). `remove(key, value?)` and `replace(key, a, b?)` take fixed optional parameters and a module sentinel, not `...rest`, which would allocate an array per call. Deviation: `MapEntry.hashCode` uses the key it copied (`this.k`), where Java reads the key of the enclosing iterator's current position (`getIntKey()`); and `MapEntry.equals` returns false where Java throws `NullPointerException` for `e.getValue() == null` with a non-null `v`. |

The boxed `forEach(BiConsumer)` is at Java's position, the first method after `Boxed Versions Below`. The key set and the value collection inherit `remove(Object)` and `clear()` from the `AbstractCollection` stand-in, as Java's do. |
| (none; port-only addition for spec D14) generated from `src/agrona/collections/Long2ObjectHashMap.ts` | `src/agrona/collections/LongHalves2ObjectHashMap.ts` | The `Long2ObjectHashMap` with the key split into two `int` halves `(hi, lo)` in an `Int32Array` (two slots per key); every keyed method takes `(hi, lo)`, each `\| 0`. Same constructor, probing, resize, load factor, `shouldAvoidAllocation`, iteration order (for a key in +/-(2^53 - 1) the slots and the order are those of `Long2ObjectHashMap`, tested). | (none; port-only addition for spec D14) generated from `src/agrona/collections/Long2LongHashMap.ts` | `src/agrona/collections/LongHalves2LongHashMap.ts` | The `Long2LongHashMap` with the key split into two `int` halves `(hi, lo)`: the keys are in an `Int32Array` (two slots per key) and the values in a `Float64Array` at the same index, where `Long2LongHashMap` interleaves both in one `Float64Array`; an empty slot is a value equal to the missing value, as there. The slot of a key is half the interleaved key index, so the probing visits the slots in the same order (`Hashing.evenHashLongHalves`, then `>> 1`) and the iteration order is that of `Long2LongHashMap` for a key in +/-(2^53 - 1) (tested). Every keyed method takes `(hi, lo)`, each `\| 0`; a value or missing value outside +/-(2^53 - 1) throws `RangeError` (D5). Iterators and the key set expose `getKeyHigh()` / `getKeyLow()` and `nextKey()`; the value iterator keeps `nextValue()`; `forEachLong` is `forEach(keyHigh, keyLow, value)`; `LongLongConsumer`, `LongLongFunction` and `LongLongPredicate` are `LongHalvesLongConsumer`, `LongHalvesLongFunction` and `LongHalvesLongPredicate` of the same file. Not ported: the boxed `Map` methods (as `Long2LongHashMap`), `longForEach`, `putAll(Map)` and `equals(Map)` against a foreign map (`putAll` takes the same class), `hashCode` and `equals` of the map, entry and entry set, `toArray`, the `AbstractSet` base of the key and entry sets, `removeIfLong` on the key set (it is `removeIfLongHalves`). |

A null key throws `NullPointerException`. Entries are one `unknown[]`, keys at even indexes. Same TS shape as `Int2ObjectHashMap` for the nested classes, `Map` methods and `V \| null`; `putAll` takes anything with `entrySet()`. |

| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/concurrent/AgentRunner.java` | `src/agrona/concurrent/AgentRunner.ts` | Runs on the event loop (spec D3, D11): `run()` calls `onStart` and returns, each duty cycle is a task on `setImmediate` (or a timer for an idle strategy's park); the `AtomicReference<Thread>` is a plain field; the constructor takes an `AgentRunnerScheduler` (the Node event loop by default; tests pass `ManualScheduler`). `close()` called from outside the agent ends the loop at once (the thread cannot be inside a duty cycle) and calls `onClose` before returning; called from inside the agent it ends the loop after the cycle. A caller that is interrupted when it calls `close()` gets the Java behaviour: the fail action is called with the runner thread, the runner thread is interrupted, and the caller's flag is restored. The retry timeout is accepted and unused (the join cannot time out). `startOnThread(runner)` has no `ThreadFactory` overload. Additions: `AgentRunnerScheduler`, `NODE_SCHEDULER`, `wake()` (spec D8). `run()` on a runner that is already running does nothing and does not mark it closed (Java's does). |

| (no Java file) | `test/extra/agrona/concurrent/broadcast/BroadcastCrossWorkerExtraTest.test.ts`, `Broadcast.worker.ts`, `BroadcastLayout.ts` | One transmitter worker sends 200k seeded messages (type id = sequence + 1, length a multiple of 4 in 0 to `maxMsgLength`) into a 1 KiB buffer; three receiver workers read them (`fast`; `stall-until-done`, which stops after 10 records until the transmitter has finished; `copy-stalling`, a `CopyBroadcastReceiver` that stalls every 997 records until the transmitter is 200 messages ahead), so the two slow ones are lapped on purpose. A record that passes `validate()` must have the right type, length and bytes and a sequence above the last; `lappedCount` must be above 0 for the slow receivers. Run from position 0, just below 2^31, just below 2^32 and 64 KiB below 2^32. The payload bytes are all in 0x80..0xFE and the lengths multiples of 4, because Java's `receiveNext` reads a record length after it validated the position, so a receiver lapped in between can read payload as a length and, if that looks like a small positive length, take a position in the middle of a record as a record start (found by this test with free payloads; upstream behaviour, not a port difference). `CopyBroadcastReceiver.receive` copies before `validate()`, so a torn length can make `putBytes` throw `IndexOutOfBoundsException` instead of `IllegalStateException`; the worker counts both as a dropped record. |

The words stay `int` pairs, not `putLong` / `putLongRelease`, because the words at +8, +16 and +24 hold arbitrary 64-bit values (the session, stream and term ids, the reserved value) that a `number` cannot hold under D5, and a striped `long` store would throw for them; ordering is preserved: the plain `putInt(+4)` (type, flags, version) happens-before the sequentially consistent `putIntRelease(0)` of the length, and a reader that sees the length with `frameLengthVolatile` sees the rest. `HeaderWriter` likewise keeps its `int` halves. |

Addition: `nanoTimeHalves(out)` (the exact value as the `[low, high]` int halves, no allocation) and the helper `epochNanosHalves(ms, subNs, out)`; channel timestamps use it (D17). |
| `agrona:agrona/src/main/java/org/agrona/concurrent/SystemEpochNanoClock.java` | `src/agrona/concurrent/SystemEpochNanoClock.ts` | `Math.floor(performance.timeOrigin * 1e6 + performance.now() * 1e6)`: no `bigint` per call (as `SystemNanoClock`). The value is about 1.8e18, above 2^53, so it is a double rounded to a multiple of 256 ns and not a safe integer (spec D17); `performance.timeOrigin` has microsecond resolution. `nanoTimeHalves(out)` is the exact form: `performance.timeOrigin` split into whole ms and sub-ms ns, plus `performance.now()`, written as int halves by `epochNanosHalves`; channel timestamps use it (D17), `nanoTime()` must never reach a `long` accessor. Unlike Java's `Instant.now()` it is monotonic and can drift from the wall clock over a long uptime. `HighResolutionClock.epochNanos()` and `OffsetEpochNanoClock` are not ported. |

A cached (or `localhost`) address holds the host name as it was asked for, as Java's lookup does (`withHostName`). `NetworkInterface.getNetworkInterfaces()` reads `os.networkInterfaces()` and returns an array, not an `Enumeration`; Node lists only interfaces that are up, gives no multicast flag (`supportsMulticast` is `!internal`) and no broadcast address (`null`); the scope of an interface name in `fe80::1%eth0` is the `scopeid` of its first IPv6 address. `createUnresolved` is the 3-argument constructor; `new InetSocketAddress(hostname, port)` resolves through `getByName` and is unresolved (name kept) on `UnknownHostException`. `NetworkInterface.getByInetAddress` is ported. IPv6 interface addresses with a scope carry the interface name (`fe80:0:0:0:0:0:0:9%eth9`). No reverse lookups (`getHostName` is the host name held or the address text). |

| `aeron:aeron-driver/src/main/java/io/aeron/driver/MediaDriver.java` | `src/driver/MediaDriver.ts` | `MediaDriver.Context` is the exported class `MediaDriverContext` (as `UdpChannel.Context` is `UdpChannelContext`), reachable as `MediaDriver.Context` for the value and the type (the outer `MediaDriver` is the launch, below). The fields have the Java names with a leading `_` because the accessors have the Java names; a getter and its fluent setter are one method; the package-private members are public. `conclude()` keeps Java's order: `concludeNullProperties`, the validations, `multiplyExact(nakUnicastDelayNs, nakUnicastRetryDelayRatio)`, `validateSessionIdRange`, then the CnC, `concludeCounters`, `concludeDependantProperties`, `concludeIdleStrategies`, the `BYTES_CURRENTLY_MAPPED` counter, the first correlation id, the heartbeat and `signalCncReady`. The CnC is one `SharedArrayBuffer` of `align(META_DATA_LENGTH + the five buffers, filePageSize)` bytes, filled with `CncFileDescriptor.fillMetaData` (spec D4); the pid is `process.pid`. Java's `try` / `catch (Exception)` around the body only rethrows unchecked and is not ported. `BYTES_CURRENTLY_MAPPED` is the CnC length (Java adds `lossReportBufferLength`, but there is no loss report file here, spec section 3). Any `ThreadingMode` is accepted by `conclude` (the default from `Configuration.threadingMode()` is `DEDICATED`, as in Java, and it creates that mode's idle strategies), but the thread factories are not ported and the launch (T18) always runs `INVOKER` (D3, D54). T18 added `unicastFlowControlSupplier`, `multicastFlowControlSupplier`, `congestControlSupplier` (the getter is `congestionControlSupplier`, as Java), the four feedback delay generators (`concludeNullProperties` makes them as Java does), `terminationValidator`, `nativeResourceAgentCommandQueue` and `nativeResourceAgentProxy` (`concludeDependantProperties` makes it over the queue with the `NATIVE_RESOURCE_AGENT_PROXY_FAILS` counter). The receiver and sender proxies and queues were added by T16 and T15, and `logFactory` by T15 (a `MemoryLogFactory`, `close()` closes it). Left out: `nativeResourceAgentIdleStrategy` and `nativeResourceAgentThreadFactory` (no threads, D3), `flowControlGroupTag` (phase 3). Out of scope: the five thread factories (D3), `aeronDirectoryName`, `deleteDirectory`, `warnIfDirectoryExists`, `dirDeleteOnStart`, `dirDeleteOnShutdown`, `useWindowsHighResTimer`, the mark file, `lossReport` and `lossReportBuffer` (the loss report file, spec section 3; `lossReportBufferLength` is ported as data and its page-aligned check in `concludeDependantProperties` is not), the `DriverNameResolver` branch of `concludeDependantProperties` (name resolution gossip, spec section 3: the `nameResolver` stays `DefaultNameResolver`; the `resolverName` check of `concludeNullProperties` is kept) and `HighResolutionTimer`. T12 added `senderPortManager` and `receiverPortManager` (`concludeNullProperties` makes a `WildcardPortManager` each from the wildcard port range strings, as Java) and `osDefaultSocketRcvbufLength`, `osMaxSocketRcvbufLength`, `osDefaultSocketSndbufLength`, `osMaxSocketSndbufLength` and `resolveOsSocketBufLengths` (public with `@internal`; the probe is a bound loopback socket, spec D31; `conclude()` calls it right after `concludeNullProperties`, as Java). T13 added `sendChannelEndpointSupplier`, `receiveChannelEndpointSupplier` and `receiveChannelEndpointThreadLocals` (`concludeNullProperties` creates them as Java does, from `Configuration` and `new ReceiveChannelEndpointThreadLocals()`), `dataTransportPoller` (`concludeDependantProperties` creates it with the counted error handler) and the accessors of `controlTransportPoller`. `toString` leaves out the omitted members and shows the two port managers. T17 added `driverCommandQueue` (`concludeNullProperties` makes the `ManyToOneConcurrentLinkedQueue`), `clientProxy` (over a `BroadcastTransmitter` of the to-clients buffer) and `driverConductorProxy` (over the command queue), both in `concludeDependantProperties` where Java makes them, and the creation of the `controlTransportPoller` there with the counted error handler and the proxy; their accessors are public with `@internal`, and `toString` shows them. T18 ported the outer class: the six `AERON_DRIVER_*_THREAD_NAME` and three `_CLASSIC` constants are static members; `launch(ctx = new Context())` (the Java `launch()` and `launch(Context)` are one) validates the socket buffer lengths (`Configuration.validateSocketBufferLengths`, as Java, before `conclude()`), concludes the context, makes the `NativeResourceAgent`, the `DriverConductor`, the `Receiver` and the `Sender`, hands them to their proxies (`nativeResourceAgent(...)`, `driverConductor(...)`, `receiver(...)`, `sender(...)`) and steps `new CompositeAgent(sender, receiver, nativeResourceAgent, conductor)` from an `AgentInvoker` that `launch` starts; a failure other than `ConcurrentConcludeException` closes the context. `context()`, `sharedAgentInvoker()`, `close()` (closes the invoker, whose `onClose` closes the agents and the context through the conductor) and `toString` (`MediaDriver{}`: there is no directory name). Not ported: `main`, `launchEmbedded`, `aeronDirectoryName`, `ensureDirectoryIsRecreated`, `reportExistingErrors`, the high resolution timer, the runners and thread factories of `SHARED`, `SHARED_NETWORK` and `DEDICATED` (D3, D54). T22 (spec D100, D101): a threading mode other than `INVOKER` also starts an `AgentRunner` over an `EmbeddedAgent` (the driver's invoker, then the hosted client conductors); `MediaDriver.runner()`, `wake()`, `host()`, `unhost()` and `MediaDriver.Context.wakeUp` are additions. |

| `aeron:aeron-driver/src/main/java/io/aeron/driver/media/UdpChannelTransport.java` | `src/driver/media/UdpChannelTransport.ts` | Abstract; a node `dgram` socket for each `DatagramChannel`, a `ReceiveRing` and a send slot (spec 4.2, D1, D2, D28). The Java fields that have an accessor of the same name are `_udpChannel`, `_receiveDatagramChannel`, `_multicastTtl`, `_socketRcvbufLength` and `_socketSndbufLength` (as `UdpChannel`); `sendDatagramChannel` and `connectAddress` keep their names. The constructor takes the buffer lengths as optional arguments (Java: two constructors), and every parameter may be `null` as in `UdpChannelTransportTest`. `openDatagramChannel` makes the sockets with a synchronous `lookup` that takes IP literals only (N2, N6), rethrows a bind error that node emitted inside `bind()` as the `AeronException` "channel error - ...", and sets the status indicator to `ERRORED` as Java does. `receive(UnsafeBuffer)` plus `receivedLength()` stand for `receive(ByteBuffer)` and `position()`. `send` and `sendTo` are added (D2, D28), `onMessage` is the `'message'` handler (public, for tests) and `setWakeUp` the D8 hook. `isValidFrame` is Java's with a `switch` statement for the `switch` expression. `onSendError` is static as in Java; the deprecated `sendError` is not ported. The hooks do nothing. Nothing is allocated per datagram on the receive and the send path once the senders are known: the source addresses are interned per transport by (host, port) in a table of `SOURCE_ADDRESS_CAPACITY` (16) entries, replaced round-robin, so only a new or evicted sender allocates an address (T8 review, review batch 2), `getHostAddress()` is memoized (`jdkNet.ts`), the callbacks are created once. T13 added `isConnected()` (Java: `sendDatagramChannel.isConnected()`; a flag set after a connect that node accepted, cleared by `updateEndpoint` and `close()`) and takes the length of the send slot from `SetupFlyweight.MAX_UDP_PAYLOAD_LENGTH`, so that the transport does not import `Configuration` (spec D32). The `wakeUp` function starts as the `MediaDriver.Context.wakeUp` of the context (T22, spec D101). |

The correlation id and position of `requestRejection`-style calls are `number`s (spec D5). |

| (no Java file) | `test/interop/InteropRig.ts`, `test/interop/UnicastTsToC.test.ts`, `test/interop/UnicastCToTs.test.ts`, `test/interop/InteropStreams.ts`, `test/interop/InteropRows.ts`, `test/interop/MulticastInterop.test.ts`, `test/interop/MdcInterop.test.ts`, `test/interop/MdsInterop.test.ts`, `test/interop/build-c.mjs`, `test/interop/c/interop_publisher.c`, `test/interop/c/interop_subscriber.c`, `test/interop/c/interop_common.h`, `vitest.interop.config.ts` | Port-only (T23 and phase 3 T12, T13, spec 8.3, D105 to D109, D116): TS against the real Aeron C 1.53.3 media driver on real UDP on the loopback, unicast, both directions (TS publication to a C subscriber, C publication to a TS subscription). Message sizes 1 B, MTU - 32, MTU - 31, 2 x MTU and term length / 8 alone and mixed; term rotation (64 KiB terms, more than eight rotations, `init-term-id` near `Integer.MAX_VALUE` so the term id wraps); loss (C interceptor and a loopback relay, with NAK and retransmit asserted); a slow consumer on each side that back pressures the other (`interop_subscriber -P <ms>` pauses polling once after the first message); a subscriber that leaves and one that joins; a publication that closes (end of stream) and a new one. Phase 3 (T12, T13, D116) added the multicast, MDC (dynamic and manual) and MDS rows. `interop_publisher` and `interop_subscriber` take `-D <destination>` (repeatable: manual MDC destinations of a publication, MDS destinations of a subscription, added with the C client's `async_add_destination` and polled to completion under the deadline) and the subscriber `-E` (also wait for an image to end, so that the end of stream is in its summary). `InteropRig` has `multicastChannel` (group 224.20.30.39, `interface=localhost`), `addPublicationWhenFree` (repeats an add that the driver refuses with "found in CLOSING state, please retry", as Aeron's contract says; this was the 1 in 6 flake of the unicast end of stream row) and a `LossyRelay` that drops on the way to a destination (`forward`) or on the way back from a dynamic MDC publisher (`return`). `InteropStreams.ts` holds the two directions (`ToReceivers`: a TS publication and C subscribers, each in a C driver of its own; `FromPublisher`: a C publication and a TS subscription) and `InteropRows.ts` the sizes and term rotation rows shared by multicast, dynamic MDC and manual MDC. Rows: TS publication to two C subscribers (MDC dynamic, MDC manual, multicast); C publication to a TS subscription (MDC dynamic, MDC manual, multicast, MDS with two destinations); MDC under loss in both directions (C interceptor on the receivers and on the publisher, relay on the data to the TS side); multicast under loss; a C subscriber that leaves and rejoins an MDC publication (connection timeout, new image, end of stream); MDS with lossy destinations and a destination removed mid-stream; every size and term rotation (more than eight terms, wrapping `init-term-id`) on MDC and multicast. The tests are not in `npm test` and have no Java counterpart, so they are not in the Tests table; `npm run test:interop` runs them. |
| (no Java file) | `bench/lib/e2e.ts`, `bench/lib/inject.ts`, `bench/e2e/report.ts`, `bench/micro/e2e.bench.ts`, `bench/micro/termreader.bench.ts`, `test/extra/bench/GateEndToEndTest.test.ts` | Port-only (T24, spec D110, D111, D112): the end-to-end benches and their harness (round trips and a one-way stream of two nodes on the loopback, the raw `dgram` floor, the first message after idle, allocation per message), `bench:e2e` (the report, `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` in CI), the micro bench of `TermReader.read` (1 and 16 fragments), the delay that the harness injects into the candidate, and the two tests of the real gate: a candidate with 20% more in `TermReader.read` fails it, the same commit passes it. Baselines in `bench/README.md`. |

| `test/agrona/concurrent/AgentRunnerTest.test.ts` | shouldInterruptRunnerThreadIfCloseCallIsItselfInterruped | needs a runner thread blocked in `doWork` (`Thread.sleep`) and a second thread that calls `close()` and is interrupted while it joins. A duty cycle on the one JS thread cannot be pre-empted and `close()` has nothing to wait for. The interrupted-caller path is covered by `AgentRunnerExtraTest` (`closeFromAnInterruptedCallerInterruptsTheRunnerAndRestoresTheCallersFlag`). |
| `test/agrona/concurrent/AgentRunnerTest.test.ts` | shouldInterruptRunnerThreadIfCloseDoesNotCompleteWithinCloseBudget | needs `doWork` to block for `Integer.MAX_VALUE` ms until `close()` times out and interrupts it, twice. A blocking duty cycle would block the event loop and the close timeout cannot expire. |

## Adapted tests

Tests whose literal values or mechanism had to change, with the same behaviour asserted. A `long` is a `number` that is
exact to +/-(2^53 - 1) (spec 5.2, D5), so `Long.MAX_VALUE` / `Long.MIN_VALUE` in a test are `jdk.ts` `Long.MAX_VALUE` /
`Long.MIN_VALUE` (2^53 - 1 and -(2^53 - 1)) unless the row says otherwise.

| TypeScript test | Test | What changed and why |
|---|---|---|
| `test/driver/DefaultMulticastFlowControlSupplierTest.test.ts` | all | `assertEquals(X.class, flowControl.getClass())` is `expect(flowControl.constructor).toBe(X)`; `assertThrows(IllegalArgumentException.class, ...)` is `assertThrows` of `test/support/Assertions.ts`; `@ValueSource` is `it.each`. |
| `test/driver/FlowControlTest.test.ts` | usesDefaultMulticastRetransmitReceiverWindowMultipleWhenNotSet, usesMulticastRetransmitReceiverWindowMultipleFromContextWhenNotSetInUri, usesMulticastRetransmitReceiverWindowMultipleFromUriWhenSet | `@MethodSource("multicastFlowControlStrategies")` is `it.each` over a name, a factory (Java makes the strategy in the stream, once per row; the factory makes it per test) and the channel, for `MaxMulticastFlowControl`, `MinMulticastFlowControl` and `TaggedMulticastFlowControl`; `mockStatic(FlowControl.class)` is the spy of `withMockedFlowControl` as in the unicast rows. |
| `test/driver/TaggedMulticastFlowControlTest.test.ts` | all | As `MinMulticastFlowControlTest`. A `Long` group tag is a `bigint | null` (the flyweights take a `bigint`, spec 5.2), the expected tag of `shouldParseValidFlowControlConfiguration` a `number` (spec D5, `Configuration.flowControlGroupTag()` is `-1`); `shouldFailWithInvalidUris` asserts `toThrow()` (Java: `Exception.class`); `ByteBuffer.allocate(n)` is a `Uint8Array`. |
| `test/driver/MinMulticastFlowControlTest.test.ts` | all | A flow control and a counters manager per test (made in the test, as Java's field initialisers run per test instance); `Tests.newCountersManager` is a local helper (as in `FlowControlTest`); the `byte[]` temp buffer is an `UnsafeBuffer` over an `ArrayBuffer`; `statusMessageFlyweight.receiverId(long)` is `receiverId(BigInt(id))` (the strategy reads the halves, D14); the `null` receiver address is `null as never`; `@MethodSource` is `it.each`. |
| `test/client/command/TerminateDriverFlyweightTest.test.ts` | tokenBuffer | `ByteBuffer.allocate(n)` is an `ArrayBuffer`; the `byte[]` filled with 1 is a `Uint8Array`; `(byte)15` is 15. |
| `test/agrona/BitUtilTest.test.ts` | shouldAlignValueToNextMultipleOfAlignmentLong | `align(Long.MAX_VALUE, 64)` is `Long.MIN_VALUE` in Java (the add overflows and wraps). The aligned value, 2^53, is not a safe integer, so `alignLong` throws `RangeError` (spec D5: a result outside +/-(2^53 - 1) throws, it never wraps), and the test asserts the throw. The assertions below it are unchanged. |
| `test/agrona/AsciiEncodingTest.test.ts` | shouldParseLong | Every Java value is kept (`0`, `-123456789876543210`, `Long.MAX_VALUE - 1`, `Long.MAX_VALUE`, `Long.MIN_VALUE + 1`, `Long.MIN_VALUE`, with Java's 2^63 - 1 bounds). `assertParsedLong` asserts the value for a long inside +/-(2^53 - 1), and for one outside it a `LongOverflowError` whose `value` is the exact long Java returns. The two safe bounds, 2^53 - 1 and -(2^53 - 1), are asserted as plain values. |
| `test/agrona/AsciiEncodingTest.test.ts` | digitCountLongValue | The values are `bigint`s (`digitCountLong` accepts `number \| bigint`), because the loop reaches `Long.MAX_VALUE` (19 digits). `>>> 1` on a positive long is `>> 1n`. The expected digit counts are the Java ones. |
| `test/agrona/AsciiEncodingTest.test.ts` | parseIntAsciiRoundTrip | `assertEquals` is `node:assert` `strictEqual` in the loop (`expect` costs seconds for that many calls), and the 1,000,000 iterations are divided by `ITERATIONS_SCALE` (see "Iteration counts" below). Same assertion. |
| `test/agrona/AsciiEncodingTest.test.ts` | parseLongAsciiRoundTrip | Java's random 64-bit long, as a `bigint` (every fourth one in full, the others shifted right by 0 to 63 bits so that every digit count occurs). Inside +/-(2^53 - 1) the parse returns the value; outside it throws `LongOverflowError` whose `value` is the generated long. `node:assert` and `ITERATIONS_SCALE`, as `parseIntAsciiRoundTrip`. `Error.stackTraceLimit` is 0 during the loop, and only every eighth value is the full-range long (the throwing branch is slow), the others are folded into +/-(2^53 - 1) (see "Iteration counts"). |
| `test/agrona/SystemUtilTest.test.ts` | shouldParseSizesWithSuffix | Java's rows are kept. A result outside +/-(2^53 - 1) (`8589934591g`, `8796093022207m`, `9007199254740991k`) throws `LongOverflowError` whose `value` is Java's result, which `assertParsedLong` asserts, with the expected values as `bigint`s. Extra rows at the 2^53 bound follow Java's: `8388607g`, `8589934591m`, `8796093022207k`, `8796093022208k`, `8388608g`. |
| `test/agrona/SystemUtilTest.test.ts` | shouldParseTimesWithSuffix | Java's rows are kept, with `bigint` expected values. A result above 2^53 - 1 saturates, in the bare number, `ns`, `us`, `ms` and `s` branches alike, at `Long.MAX_VALUE` of `jdk.ts`, 2^53 - 1, where Java saturates at 2^63 - 1 (spec D5); `assertDuration` expects that for every Java value above it. Extra rows at the 2^53 bound: `9007199254740991`, `9007199254740991ns`, `9007199254740us`, `9007199254ms`, `9007199s`, `9007199254741us`, `9007199255ms`, `9007200s`. |
| `test/agrona/SystemUtilTest.test.ts` | shouldReturnLongMaxValueIfExceedsForSuffix | Java's rows are kept, expecting Java's `Long.MAX_VALUE` (2^63 - 1) as 2^53 - 1 for every suffix, `ns` included (saturation, spec D5). |
| `test/agrona/SystemUtilTest.test.ts` | formatDurationShouldRejectNegativeValues | `Long.MIN_VALUE` is -(2^53 - 1). |
| `test/agrona/SystemUtilTest.test.ts` | shouldFormatDuration | The `Long.MAX_VALUE` row uses 2^53 - 1. |
| `test/agrona/SystemUtilTest.test.ts` | formatSizeShouldRejectNegativeValues | `Long.MIN_VALUE` is -(2^53 - 1). |
| `test/agrona/SystemUtilTest.test.ts` | shouldFormatSize | The rows for `Long.MAX_VALUE` and for the largest multiples of `g`, `m`, `k` use 2^53 - 1 and the largest multiples under it (`8388607g`, `8589934591m`, `8796093022207k`). |
| `test/agrona/AsciiEncodingTest.test.ts` | shouldDetectEightDigitsAsciiEncodedNumbers | The eight bytes are read with `getLongBigInt` (`isEightDigitAsciiEncodedNumber` takes a `bigint`, 8 ASCII digits are above 2^53 - 1), and the 810,811-iteration loop uses `node:assert` instead of `expect`, which costs about 10 s for that many calls. Same assertions. |
| `test/agrona/AsciiEncodingTest.test.ts` | shouldParseEightDigitsFromAnAsciiEncodedNumberInLittleEndianByteOrder | As above: `getLongBigInt`, `node:assert`. |
| `test/agrona/MutableDirectBufferTests.ts` | putIntAsciiRoundTrip | `assertEquals` is `node:assert` `strictEqual` in the loop (`expect` costs seconds for that many calls), and the 10,000,000 iterations are divided by `ITERATIONS_SCALE`. Same assertion. |
| `test/agrona/MutableDirectBufferTests.ts` | putLongAsciiRoundTrip | The full range of a Java long, as a `bigint` (see `AsciiEncodingTest.parseLongAsciiRoundTrip`). `putLongAscii` takes a `number`, so inside +/-(2^53 - 1) the long is put and parsed back; outside it, its digits are written with `putStringWithoutLengthAscii` and `parseLongAscii` must throw `LongOverflowError` whose `value` is the long. `node:assert` and `ITERATIONS_SCALE`. |
| `test/agrona/MutableDirectBufferTests.ts` | putNaturalIntAsciiRoundTrip | `node:assert` and `ITERATIONS_SCALE`, as `putIntAsciiRoundTrip`. |
| `test/agrona/MutableDirectBufferTests.ts` | putNaturalLongAsciiRoundTrip | `nextLong(0, Long.MAX_VALUE)` is a random natural 63-bit long of Java as a `bigint`, handled as in `putLongAsciiRoundTrip` with `parseNaturalLongAscii`. `node:assert` and `ITERATIONS_SCALE`. |
| `test/agrona/MutableDirectBufferTests.ts` | putLongAsciiShouldEncodeBoundaryValues | `Long.MIN_VALUE` / `Long.MAX_VALUE` are -(2^53 - 1) / 2^53 - 1. The `Long.MIN_VALUE` branch of `putLongAscii` (negating it overflows) does not exist for a `number`. |
| `test/agrona/MutableDirectBufferTests.ts` | getBytesShouldCopyIntoDestinationBuffer | `ExpandableDirectByteBuffer` is not ported (it is a direct `ByteBuffer`); the `UnsafeBuffer` rows over a heap and a direct `ByteBuffer` are an `UnsafeBuffer` over a `Uint8Array` and over an `ArrayBuffer`. `ExpandableArrayBuffer` is Java's. A `byte` is read back signed: `data[i]` is converted with `(byte)`. |
| `test/agrona/MutableDirectBufferTests.ts` | putBytesShouldCopyBytesFromTheSourceBuffer | As `getBytesShouldCopyIntoDestinationBuffer`. |

| `test/agrona/collections/ArrayUtilTest.test.ts` | all four | The `Integer` constants are numbers (`===` is Java's reference equality for the cached `Integer`s 1 to 3); `assertArrayEquals` is `toEqual`; `ArrayUtil.remove(values, 0)` is `ArrayUtil.removeAt`. |
| `test/agrona/collections/Int2ObjectHashMapTest.test.ts` | all | `assertThat(x, is(y))` / `equalTo` / `nullValue()` are `toBe` / `toBeNull`; the test's `HashSet`s are `Set`s (and `assertEquals(set, set)` is `toEqual`), the `ArrayList`s arrays; a lambda that Java passes as `IntObjectToObjectFunction` or `BiFunction` is an arrow function. | `test/agrona/concurrent/AgentRunnerTest.test.ts` | shouldReportExceptionThrownByAgent, shouldNotReportClosedByInterruptException, shouldNotReportRethrownClosedByInterruptException | `new Thread(runner).start()` is `runner.run()`; a `CountDownLatch` is a counter that the test drives the scheduler until it reaches 0 (`latch.await(3, SECONDS)` is the capped `runUntil`). |
| `test/agrona/concurrent/AgentRunnerTest.test.ts` | shouldNotInterruptRunnerThreadIfCloseCompletesOnTime | The agent's `Thread.sleep(50)` is dropped (a cycle is not pre-empted) and `close(300, ...)` is called between cycles. The assertions are the same: no interrupt of the caller, the runner thread dead, the work finished, the runner thread not interrupted (`runnerThread.isInterrupted()` after `close()`, and the flag seen in `doWork`), zero fail actions. |
| `test/agrona/concurrent/AgentRunnerTest.test.ts` | shouldInterruptRunnerThreadIfCloseCompletesOnTimeWhenMainThreadIsInterruptedBeforeTheCloseCall | The agent's `Thread.sleep(50)` is dropped. Its `interrupted` flag (the sleep was interrupted) is the runner thread's `isInterrupted()` after `close()`. The caller's flag is restored, the fail action is called once, the runner thread is dead. |
| `test/agrona/MutableDirectBufferTests.ts`, `test/agrona/concurrent/UnsafeBufferTest.test.ts`, `AtomicBufferTest.test.ts` | (all) | Run a second time over a `SharedArrayBuffer` (spec D16) through the `Backing` factory of `test/support/Backing.ts` (`describe.each(BACKINGS)`), with no copy of the tests. Only the buffer-creation points vary: the `byte[]` of a test (`US_ASCII.encode`, the `Uint8Array` fixtures that are compared with or wrapped as they are) keeps its meaning and stays a `Uint8Array` over its own `ArrayBuffer`; `allocateDirectAligned` is `backing.aligned` (`allocateShared` for the second run). Every access of these suites is naturally aligned, so no test needed a variant that asserts the unaligned-atomic throw; that is `anAtomicAccessAtAnAddressThatIsNotAMultipleOfItsSizeThrowsOnASharedBuffer` in `test/extra/agrona/concurrent/SharedAtomicsExtraTest.test.ts`. |
| `test/agrona/concurrent/AgentInvokerTest.test.ts`, `AgentRunnerTest.test.ts`, `CompositeAgentTest.test.ts` | (all) | `mock(Agent.class)` and `mock(ErrorHandler.class)` are the hand fakes of `test/support/MockAgent.ts` (`vi.fn()` per method, `doWork` returns 0); `verify(m).x()` is `toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1)`; `verifyNoMoreInteractions(mockAgent)` is the exact call count of each method, `roleName` included; `reset(mockAgent)` is `resetAgent`. |
| `test/agrona/concurrent/status/CountersManagerTest.test.ts` | shouldStoreMultipleLabels, shouldFreeAndReuseCounters, shouldStoreMetaData, shouldStoreRawData | `InOrder` with `verifyNoMoreInteractions` is an equality on the whole `mock.calls` list; `ArgumentCaptor` is the argument taken from `mock.calls`. `reader.forEach(metaData)` is `forEachMetaData` (rename). The `0xF_FFFF_FFFFL` value is `0xf_ffff_ffff`. |

| `test/client/FragmentAssemblerTest.test.ts` | shouldAssembleTwoPartMessage, shouldAssembleFourPartMessage, shouldPassThroughUnfragmentedMessage | `verify(delegate, times(1)).onFragment(...)` with `ArgumentCaptor` is exact `mock.calls`: one call, with the offset and the length compared and the captured header (the same object the builder reuses) read afterwards. `(byte)(UNFRAGMENTED \| 0x3)` is `((UNFRAGMENTED \| 0x3) << 24) >> 24`. |
| `test/client/FragmentAssemblerTest.test.ts` | shouldFreeSessionBuffer, shouldDoNotingIfEndArrivesWithoutBegin, shouldDoNotingIfMidArrivesWithoutBegin, shouldSkipOverMessagesWithLoss | `when(header.flags()).thenReturn(a).thenReturn(b)` on a `spy(Header)` is the flyweight over the header's buffer, whose flags are set before each `onFragment` call (a Mockito sequence also feeds `completeHeader`'s own `header.flags()`; here the flags are those of the fragment, so the assembled flags are the same as Java's except in `shouldFreeSessionBuffer`, where Java's delegate sees `0 \| END` (the bytes of the mocked header carry no flags, so `captureHeader` copies none) and here it sees `BEGIN \| END` (the header bytes carry the flags of the fragment); the test does not look at the flags). `verify(never())` is `not.toHaveBeenCalled()`. In `shouldSkipOverMessagesWithLoss` the frame headers carry term offset 0 and frame length 0 as in Java, so the END fragment is assembled too (offset 0, length 512, as in Java): `verify(times(1)).onFragment(any(), eq(offset), eq(length), ...)` is the calls filtered by that offset and length, which must be one. |
| `test/client/logbuffer/TermReaderTest.test.ts` | all | The term buffer is real, not a mock: `when(termBuffer.getIntVolatile(x)).thenReturn(len)` and `getShort(typeOffset(x))` are the frame written at `x`, so the order and arguments of the reads are checked on the `getIntVolatile` spy and the type is read by `isPaddingFrame` for real. The unstubbed reads are the zeros of a new buffer, as Mockito's 0. |
| `test/client/logbuffer/TermReaderTest.test.ts` | shouldReadOneLimitedMessage | `getIntVolatile(anyInt())` returning the frame length is a run of frames at every aligned offset; `verifyNoMoreInteractions` is the call counts. |

| `test/client/logbuffer/TermRebuilderTest.test.ts` | all | The term buffer is real. `verify(termBuffer).putLong(termOffset + 24, packet.getLong(24))` and the two after it are the six `putInt(termOffset + 28 .. 8, packet.getInt(..))` calls, upper half of each word first (spec D5), then `putInt(termOffset + 4, ..)`, and `putLongRelease(termOffset, packet.getLong(0))` is `putIntRelease(termOffset, packet.getInt(0))`. The packets carry a byte pattern after the frame length (Java's are zeros) so that the copy is checked on the bytes of the term buffer, not only on the calls. |

| `test/client/logbuffer/LogBufferUnblockerTest.test.ts` | shouldUnblockWhenPositionHasNonCommittedMessageAndTailPastEndOfTerm | `putLong(TERM_TAIL_COUNTER_OFFSET, pack(TERM_ID_1, TERM_LENGTH + HEADER_LENGTH))` is `putLongBigInt(.., packTail(..))`. |

The consumer's `Thread.yield()` loop is the poll loop. |
