## Golden wire vectors

`test/golden/goldenFrames.test.ts` is not a port of a Java test, so it has no row in **Tests**. It checks the flyweights
and `FrameDescriptor` alignment against bytes that two real Aeron 1.53.3 **C** drivers exchanged (plan T13):

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `scripts/capture-golden.mjs` | Runs `aeronmd` x2, `basic_publisher`/`streaming_publisher` and `basic_subscriber` in five scenarios (`basic`, `fragmented`, `pad`, `loss`, `setup-elicit`) through a loopback UDP relay that records every datagram, drops one chosen DATA frame in `loss`, and decodes each frame with an independent `DataView` decoder (offsets from `aeron_udp_protocol.h`). It keeps the first 12 datagrams of each frame class per scenario (the RTT measurement alone produces thousands) and always keeps PAD, NAK and SETUP. |
| `test/golden/frames.json` | The committed capture: 263 datagrams. Frame kinds: SETUP, SM plain, SM with `SEND_SETUP` (0x80), SM with the end-of-stream flag (0x40), DATA unfragmented (0xc0), BEGIN (0x80) / MIDDLE (0x00) / END (0x40) fragments of a 3500 byte message at MTU 1408, PAD at term end (a PAD header follows the last data frame in the same datagram), heartbeat (0xc0, frame length 0), EOS heartbeat (0xe0), NAK, the retransmitted DATA, RTTM request and reply. |
| `test/golden/goldenFrames.test.ts` | Per datagram: walks the frames with `FrameDescriptor.FRAME_ALIGNMENT`, decodes with the flyweights and compares to the recorded fields, re-encodes into a fresh zeroed buffer and compares the bytes. A coverage block asserts every type and flag above against the C header literals (and the per-frame `flagNames` the capture script derives from its own copy of the C flag constants); a constants test pins every TS `HDR_TYPE_*` and flag to those literals. Needs no C driver. |

To re-run the capture, see the README ("Golden wire vectors").

Not in these flows, so covered by their flyweight tests only: ERR, RSP_SETUP, RES, ATS frames.

Facts the capture showed about the 1.53.3 C driver, which the flyweight tests did not pin down:

- A heartbeat is a bare 32 byte DATA header whose `frameLength` field is `0`, not `32` (`DataHeaderFlyweight.isHeartbeat`).
- A PAD frame is sent as its 32 byte header only; its `frameLength` covers the whole pad to the end of the term.
- The C receiver's RTTM **request** carries `REPLY_FLAG` (0x80, "please reply"); the sender's **reply** has the flag clear.
- The C sender puts the PAD header in the same datagram as the preceding data frame(s).

Fields the stock C samples never vary, so the golden vectors only show one value and the flyweight unit tests alone cover the rest: `reservedValue` (always 0), RTTM `receptionDelta` (always 0), `version` (always 0) and a negative `frameLength`. Also not varied: the pinned session id, stream id and term ids (one value each per capture).

The capture pins `sessionId` to a negative int (0x87654321), `streamId` to 0x12345678 and starts the publication one term after the initial term, so SETUP has `activeTermId` = `initialTermId` + 1. `frames.json` records, per scenario, the NAKs the deliberate drop did not cause (`unintendedNaks`: the relay can drop datagrams under a burst); the NAK checks are scoped to the deliberate drop.

Flyweight disagreements with the real bytes: none found. (No `it.skip` was needed.)

## Iteration counts

The Java tests run their random round trips for 1,000,000 (`AsciiEncodingTest`) and 10,000,000 (`MutableDirectBufferTests`) iterations. Both loops run `count / ITERATIONS_SCALE` iterations, where `test/support/Iterations.ts` has `ITERATIONS_SCALE = process.env.AERON_TS_FULL_ITERATIONS ? 1 : 100`. CI sets `AERON_TS_FULL_ITERATIONS=1` on the Node 24 job only.

So that the full-size round trips fit in `ROUND_TRIP_TIMEOUT_MS` (180 s), `putLongAsciiRoundTrip`, `putNaturalLongAsciiRoundTrip` and `parseLongAsciiRoundTrip` set `Error.stackTraceLimit = 0` during the loop (restored in `finally`) and take the overflow (throwing) branch on one iteration in eight only: on `i % 8 === 0` the value is the full-range `randomJavaLong(i >> 3)`, on the others `randomJavaLong(i)` folded into the safe range with `BigInt.asIntN(53, ...)` (`asUintN(53, ...)` for the natural one) when outside it. The overflow assertion is unchanged.

## Deviations

The deviations of this port that touch these files (the full list is spec section 6):

| # | Deviation |
|---|---|
| D1 | Receive: `'message'` events fill a bounded per-transport ring (byte budget = `SO_RCVBUF`, overflow counted in `RECEIVE_RING_DROPS`); `receive()` pops one datagram per poll. See spec D1. |
| D2 | Send: copy into a per-socket send slot; a non-empty libuv send queue returns 0; an asynchronous send error is reported at the next duty cycle and counts as a lost datagram. See spec D2. |
| D3 | `INVOKER` threading, plus the event loop runner of D100 for the other modes; `DEDICATED` and `SHARED_NETWORK` have no threads of their own. See spec D3. |
| D4 | Log buffers and counters live in memory; no CnC or `.logbuffer` files. See spec D4. |
| D5 | Every Java `long` is a `number` in +/-(2^53 - 1). A result or stored value outside that range throws `RangeError` (`LongOverflowError` when it is a parsed or stored long, carrying the exact value) and writes nothing; it never wraps. It covers get / put / getAndAdd / add / compareAndSet of longs, `putLongAscii`, `parseLongAscii`, `parseSize` / `parseDuration` and the `BitUtil` long helpers. The one exception is a saturating function: Java's `parseDuration` saturates at `Long.MAX_VALUE`, here at 2^53 - 1. |
| D5 (commands) | The `long` client, correlation, registration and position fields of the command flyweights are `number`s in +/-(2^53 - 1) (spec 5.2): they are allocated from `nextCorrelationId` and counted, so 2^53 needs 2^53 commands. `PublicationErrorFrameFlyweight.receiverId` and `groupTag` are the exception: Java's receiver id is a random 64-bit value (UUID bits XOR, incremented), so they are `bigint` as in `ErrorFlyweight`. The one flags word (`RemovePublicationFlyweight`) is read and written as its low `int` (bit 0), so a peer's word outside the range never throws and the high half stays byte-identical. |
| D4, D15 (CnC) | `CncFileDescriptor` lays the CnC out in a `SharedArrayBuffer`; the file, `MappedByteBuffer` and file name members are not ported (see **Not ported**). |
| `static length` | `RemoveMessageFlyweight.length()` and `RemovePublicationFlyweight.length()` are static methods named `length`, which hide the `Function.length` of the class (TypeScript accepts it with `override`). A subclass has its own `length` data property, which hides an inherited static method of that name, so `RemoveSubscriptionFlyweight` declares `static override length()` itself (Java inherits it). |
| D9 | No `java.nio.ByteBuffer` and no raw addresses: buffers wrap an `ArrayBuffer` or a `Uint8Array`. `verifyAlignment` checks `byteOffset + addressOffset` of the window in its `ArrayBuffer`. |
| D10 (collections) | `java.util` (`Iterator`, `Map.Entry`, `AbstractCollection`, `AbstractSet`, `NoSuchElementException`, `Objects.equals` / `hashCode`, `Arrays.copyOf`, `Float.toString`) lives in `src/agrona/jdkUtil.ts`, next to `jdk.ts`. A key or value is hashed and compared through a `hashCode()` / `equals()` protocol (`HashCoded`), as `String`, `Integer` / `Double`, `Boolean`, `Long` for the primitives, and by identity (`WeakMap` hash, `===`) for any other object. `jdkUtil.ts` is a second JDK stand-in file beside `jdk.ts`; `String.hashCode` is computed on every call (a JS string caches none); a `number` key is hashed as an `Integer` if it is an int32 and as a `Double` otherwise. |
| D5 (collections) | `Long2*` maps hold `long` keys (and `Long2LongHashMap` values) as `number` in `Float64Array`s. A key or value that a mutator would store outside +/-(2^53 - 1) throws `RangeError` and changes nothing; lookups (`get`, `containsKey`, `remove`) never throw, an unsafe key is just not found. `Hashing.hashLong` is exact over the whole safe range (a `bigint` reference test), `Hashing.compoundKey` throws `LongOverflowError`. The `int` maps do not check: every keyed method normalises the key with `key | 0`, Java's `(int)` cast at the call site, so a key past 32 bits is the same wrapped key in `put`, `get`, `containsKey` and `remove`. A `-0` key or value of a `Long*` map is stored as 0. |
| D10 | The JDK exception classes live in `src/agrona/jdk.ts`, not in `exceptions.ts`; the stand-ins added by the agents and counters (`Thread`, `Charset`, `AutoCloseable`, `InterruptedException`, ...) are in `src/agrona/jdkLang.ts`. |
| D11 | Agents run on the event loop. `AgentRunner.run()` returns after `onStart`; each duty cycle is a task on `setImmediate`, or on a timer for a park. Idle strategies do not block: `idle()` returns the wait in nanoseconds (spin and yield 0, park the period; the timer is `ceil(ns / 1e6)` ms, at least 1). `Thread.currentThread()` is a logical thread with an interrupt flag (the main thread, or the runner's during its cycles). `close()` of a runner from outside the agent ends it at once. |
| D12 | Log buffer raw tails are `bigint`, with `int`-halves forms for the per-message paths. See spec D12. |
| D13 | The packed outcomes of `TermReader` and `TermScanner` are `number`s in a smaller layout, with the ranges and preconditions of spec D13. |
| D13 (`LossDetector`) | `LossDetector.pack(rebuildOffset, lossFound)` is `rebuildOffset * 2 + (lossFound ? 1 : 0)` (Java: `((long)rebuildOffset << 32) \| lossFound`); `lossFound(x)` is the low bit and `rebuildOffset(x)` is `x >>> 1`. `pack` throws `RangeError` for an offset that is not in [0, 2^31) and never wraps. The outcome is below 2^31 + 2 for the largest term (2^30) and a small integer for a 64 KiB term. Implemented as phase 2 task T11. |
| D14 | Phase 2 keys the status message maps by `(hi, lo)` `int` halves, not `number`s (`compoundKey` overflows, `receiverId` is 64 bits): `LongHalves2ObjectHashMap`, `LongHalves2LongHashMap`, `Hashing.hashLongHalves`, and `receiverIdHigh` / `receiverIdLow` on the three flyweights. See spec D14. |
| D12 | Log buffer raw tails are `bigint` (a term id is any `int`, so the packed 64-bit value cannot be a `number`): `LogBufferDescriptor.packTail`, `rawTail`, `rawTailVolatile`, `casRawTail`, `termId(rawTail)`, `termOffset(rawTail[, termLength])`; the slot is two little-endian int halves, `rawTailTermId`, `rawTailTermOffset` and `initialiseTailWithTermId` use the halves without a `bigint`. A `bigint` stored as a raw tail (the `rawTail` and `rawTailVolatile` setters and the update of `casRawTail`, checked before the compare) goes through `Long.checkInt64` (D5): outside [-2^63, 2^63 - 1] it throws `RangeError` and writes nothing. `HeaderWriter` keeps its three words as `int`s. `LogBufferDescriptor.correlationId`, `entityTag`, `responseCorrelationId` and `Header.reservedValue` are `bigint` (opaque 64-bit values). `getAndAddRawTailOffset` is the per-message `getAndAddLong` on the raw tail as two atomic `int` adds with a carry, without a `bigint`. |
| D13 | The `long` that `TermReader.pack` and `TermScanner.pack` pack two `int`s into is a `number` in a smaller layout, so that `read` and `scanForAvailability` allocate nothing. The layouts, ranges, `RangeError` cases, preconditions (a scan of 2^21 bytes or more throws; the six argument `read` needs a frame-aligned `termOffset` and a valid term) and the phase 2 items (the `LossDetector` packing, done in T11 as the next row; the cap on positions, `LogBufferDescriptor.maxPossiblePosition`) are in the design spec D13, which is the one text. A value above 2^31 is boxed when it crosses a call that is not inlined (see the V8 note below); a typical `TermReader` outcome (30 fragments or fewer) is below it. |
| D17 | `EpochNanoClock.nanoTime()` returns a `number` for the nanoseconds since the epoch (about 1.8e18, above 2^53). `SystemEpochNanoClock` rounds to a multiple of 256 ns and the value is not a safe integer; the exact value is the addition `nanoTimeHalves(out)` (int halves), used by the channel timestamps. See spec D17. |
| D18 | A `Throwable` is printed as `toString()` plus V8's frames (`\tat ...`) and the `Caused by:` chain, from `error.stack`; `DistinctErrorLog` compares errors by constructor, message, cause chain and frame text. See spec D18. |
| D19 | The Agrona concurrent classes that Java makes thread safe (`DistinctErrorLog.record`, `ManyToOneConcurrentLinkedQueue`, `ConcurrentCountersManager`) are for one thread. See spec D19. |
| D7 | Host names are resolved with `dns.lookup` before a command reaches the conductor; `DefaultNameResolver.resolve` stays synchronous and answers IP literals, `localhost` and the cache (`DefaultNameResolver.cache` / `clearCache`, stored in `InetAddress.nameCache`); an uncached name is `UnknownHostException` in `InetAddress.getByName` and `null` from `resolve`, as in Java. See spec D7. |
| D8 | `AgentRunner.wake()` on datagram arrival, send-queue drain and facade calls (the hooks are D101). See spec D8. |
| D15 | The client to driver command protocol runs in memory: the CnC is a `SharedArrayBuffer` handed from `MediaDriver` to `Aeron.Context`, and `LogBuffers` finds a log by name. See spec D15. |
| D16 | Shared memory and 64-bit atomics: a `SharedArrayBuffer` with `Atomics` for `int` and smaller, a striped lock table (`LongStripes`) for `long`; plain `long` accesses stay plain and can tear. See spec D16. |
| D20 | The `java.net` classes of the driver's address code are stand-ins in `src/driver/jdkNet.ts`. See spec D20. |
| D21 | `VarHandle.releaseFence()` and `acquireFence()` are the empty function on x64 and `Atomics.add` on other architectures; the ordering that gives on ARM64 with and without LSE is stated there. See spec D21. |
| D22 | System properties are a module-level `Map<string, string>` (`System` in `jdkLang.ts`, `systemProperties` in `jdk.ts`) that starts empty; `process.env` is never read. `Integer.getInteger`, `Long.getLong` and `Boolean.getBoolean` read it with Java's semantics. See spec D22. |
| D23 | `multiplyExact(a, b)` (`Math.multiplyExact(long, long)`) computes with `bigint`: outside Java's `long` an `ArithmeticException("long overflow")`, inside it but outside +/-(2^53 - 1) a `LongOverflowError`. See spec D23. |
| D24 | A type that is not ported yet, in a signature of a ported method, is a placeholder interface exported from the using file (`DriverConductor`, `PublicationLike`, `RawLog` in `PublicationParams.ts`); a member whose type is not ported is left out and added by the task that ports the type. See spec D24 and the **Sources** rows. |
| D25 | `Class.forName(name).getConstructor().newInstance()` has no equivalent: `Configuration.agentIdleStrategy` maps the aliases and Agrona's class names and throws `ClassNotFoundException` for any other name. See spec D25. |
| D26 | `CongestionControl.packOutcome`, `receiverWindowLength` and `shouldForceStatusMessage` pack the window length and the force bit as the `number` `receiverWindowLength * 2 + force`, where Java packs `(flags << 32) | receiverWindowLength` into a `long` (above 2^53). `packOutcome` throws `RangeError` for a window length that is not an `int` (Java would sign-extend it into the flag bits); `FORCE_STATUS_MESSAGE_BIT` is still `0x1`. See spec D26. |
| D27 | `BroadcastReceiver.lappedCount` is a plain `number` field, not an `AtomicLong`. See spec D27. |
| D28 | `UdpChannelTransport` wraps node `dgram` sockets: `receive(UnsafeBuffer)` and `receivedLength()`, `send` and `sendTo` on the transport, `setWakeUp`, one held receive error and one held send error per poll, a synchronous check of `connect`, multicast send options set after the connect, hooks that do nothing, idempotent `close()`. See spec D28. |
| D29 | `RECEIVE_RING_DROPS`, a system counter of id 46 that Aeron does not have, and the sizing of the `ReceiveRing`. See spec D29. |
| D30 | `TransportPoller` has no `Selector`: it is the list of registered transports. See spec D30. |
| D31 | The OS socket buffer lengths come from a bound loopback probe socket; `conclude()` creates the wildcard port managers. See spec D31. |
| D32 | The channel endpoints send and receive `UnsafeBuffer` windows (`send(buffer, offset, length)`, `sendTo(...)`, `sendToImageConnections`, `sendToSource`, `sendToChannel`, `isConnected()`); the send exceptions are the transport's; `UdpChannelTransport` takes its send slot length from `SetupFlyweight.MAX_UDP_PAYLOAD_LENGTH` to keep the modules free of a cycle. See spec D32. |
| D33 | Receiver ids are halves on the status message path (`Destination`, `ReceiveChannelEndpoint`); the group tag and the RTT timestamps are `bigint`s made once or passed in; `Long.checkInt64` holds its limits in constants. See spec D33. |
| D34 | `Int2IntCounterMap` and `LongHalves2LongCounterMap` are the reference counts of the receive channel endpoint, over built-in maps. See spec D34. |
| D35 | The log factory is `MemoryLogFactory`; a log is a `SharedArrayBuffer` registered by name. See spec D35. |
| D36 | `isValidStatusMessage` and `resend` take a position outside +/-(2^53 - 1) as outside the window. See spec D36. |
| D37 | `NetworkPublication.producerPosition` reads the raw tail as halves, no `bigint`. See spec D37. |
| D38 | The sender sends `UnsafeBuffer` windows; `RawLog.sliceTerms` is not ported. See spec D38. |
| D39 | Padding classes merged, `volatile` plain, `_` fields, `ReceiverLivenessTracker` keyed by halves. See spec D39. |
| D40 | `ClientCommandAdapter` does not dispatch `ADD_COUNTER`, `REMOVE_COUNTER`, `ADD_STATIC_COUNTER` and `GET_NEXT_AVAILABLE_SESSION_ID`, and `ClientProxy` has no counter or next-session-id response: those commands take the `default` branch, an `UNKNOWN_COMMAND_TYPE_ID` error response (`command typeId=<id>`). See spec D40. |
| D41 | `ClientCommandAdapter` calls `validateLength` for `REMOVE_PUBLICATION`; Java does not and reads the fields of a short message past its end. A client older than the flags field (24 bytes) is still valid. See spec D41. |
| D42 | `CommandProxy.offer` on a full queue counts the failure once and throws `IllegalStateException`; Java spins until the consumer makes room. See spec D42. |
| D43 | The conductor (and publications and images) that a T17 class calls are narrow interfaces exported from the using file (`LinkedPublication`, `SubscriptionLinkPublication`, `SubscriptionLinkImage`; the conductor interfaces `ClientCommandConductor`, `DriverConductorProxyTarget`, `AeronClientConductor`, `SubscriptionLinkConductor` were deleted by T18: the types are `DriverConductor`), and the overloads of `SubscriptionLink.matches` and the two constructors of `PublicationLink` are separate methods or one constructor. See spec D43. |
| D44 | `ClientCommandAdapter` passes `index + tokenBufferOffset()` to `onTerminateDriver`; Java passes the offset relative to the message, which is wrong for a message that does not start at 0. See spec D44. |
| D50 | No loss report file: `MediaDriver.Context.lossReport()` is `null` unless the application sets one; `LossReport` is ported over any `AtomicBuffer`. See spec D50. |
| D51 | `PublicationImage` takes the `DriverConductor` (T18 deleted the interface `PublicationImageConductor`); the placeholders of `DataPacketDispatcher.ts` are gone. See spec D51. |
| D52 | `PublicationImage.insertPacket` counts a position outside +/-(2^53 - 1) as an over run or under run instead of throwing. See spec D52. |
| D53 | `PublicationImage.validatePacket` aligns a frame length without the `int` wrap of `BitUtil.align`. See spec D53. |
| D54 | `DriverConductor` and `MediaDriver` are the network parts only (no IPC, spy, counters, `DriverLog`; the commands that name them are `INVALID_CHANNEL`); the driver always runs as `INVOKER`; no Aeron directory. See spec D54. |
| D55 | A full sender or receiver queue never leaves a half-registered resource: the conductor checks the room first, waits or refuses a removal. See spec D55. |
| D56 | `DriverConductor.onClose` does not `force()` the CnC. See spec D56. |
| D57 | No `ON_COUNTER_READY` for the client heartbeat counter; `unavailableCounter` does nothing. See spec D57. |
| D58 | `NativeResourceAgent` re-resolves a DNS name with `dns.lookup` and completes the `CommandResult` in the callback. See spec D58. |
| D59 | The supplier readers of `Configuration` have no class loader (D25, D118); the flow and congestion control files use `import type { MediaDriver }`. See spec D59. |
| D60 | The loss generators key their per stream state by `(streamId, sessionId)` in a `LongHalves2ObjectHashMap<MutableInteger>`; `BiInt2ObjectMap` is not ported. See spec D60. |
| D61 | `java.util.Random` is the `Random` of `src/agrona/jdkRandom.ts`: Java's 48 bit generator on two 24 bit halves, so a seed replays Java's `nextDouble` sequence. See spec D61. |
| D62 | `LossGenerator.shouldDropFrame` has Java's two overloads as one method told apart by `undefined === sessionId`; the long form of the generators with no long form of their own delegates to the short one. See spec D62. |
| D63 | `Configuration.sendChannelEndpointSupplier` and `receiveChannelEndpointSupplier` return the debug suppliers for the Java class names `io.aeron.driver.ext.Debug{Send,Receive}ChannelEndpointSupplier`; the debug endpoints' `send` and `sendTo` take `(buffer, offset, length)`. See spec D63. |
| D80 | The T20 classes take Java's names with these shapes: one `offer(buffer, offset?, length?, reservedValueSupplier?)`, `offerTwoBuffers` and `offerVectored`; one `removeDestination` and `asyncRemoveDestination` with a `string \| number` parameter; fields that share a name with a method have a leading `_`; the package-private members the conductor and the tests use are public and `@internal`; `Subscription.images()` is the held array (a snapshot, replaced never changed). See spec D80. |
| D81 | `ReservedValueSupplier.get` returns `bigint \| number`; a `number` is written with `putLong` and a `bigint` after `Long.checkInt64`, out of range is a `RangeError` before the frame is committed. See spec D81. |
| D82 | `ExclusivePublication` stores the raw tail with `putLongVolatileHalves` and `offerBlock` copies the first 32 bytes of the block without a `long`, the frame length last with `putIntRelease`. See spec D82. |
| D83 | Replaced by T19: `PublicationClientConductor`, `PublicationLogBuffers`, `ImageLogBuffers` and `SubscriptionClientConductor` are gone; the classes take the real `ClientConductor` and `LogBuffers` (`import type`). `AvailableImageHandler` and `UnavailableImageHandler` stay one-method interfaces. See spec D83, D96. |
| D84 | `ConcurrentPublication` claims the tail with `getAndAddRawTailOffset` and reads it with `getLongVolatileHalves`, no `bigint`; `int` term id arithmetic with `\| 0`; the cap of D13 gives `MAX_POSITION_EXCEEDED`. See spec D84. |
| D85 | `Image` masks a position with `position & termLengthMask` (exact for any position below 2^53); `fileChannel()` is `null`. See spec D85. |
| D90 | `NullValue.ts` holds `NULL_VALUE` for the files that `Aeron.ts` imports; `Aeron.NULL_VALUE` is the same value. The nested classes `Aeron.Context` and `Aeron.Configuration` are `AeronContext` and `AeronConfiguration`. See spec D90. |
| D91 | `LogBuffers` is over the in-memory logs of the driver, found by name; windows of its own; `MemoryLogBuffersFactory`; `fileChannel()` is `null`. See spec D91. |
| D92 | A host name in the channel of an `async` call is looked up before the command is written: the registration id is allocated at once, the command is written by a duty cycle after the look-ups; a remove cancels it. The commands reach the driver in the order they were made: while one waits, every later command of an `async` call is queued behind it, and the queue is written from its head; a synchronous call writes the queue first. `Aeron.resolveChannelHosts` for the synchronous calls. See spec D92. |
| D93 | `Aeron.Context` takes the CnC, the logs and the invoker from a `MediaDriver` of the process (`mediaDriver`), with no directory and no waiting for the heartbeat; the default lock is a count; the default error handler does not exit the process. See spec D93. |
| D94 | The client conductor never sleeps or parks: no sleep in `onClose`, none in `awaitResponse`. See spec D94. |
| D95 | The counters API, the counter handlers and `nextAvailableSessionId` are not ported in the client; `nextSessionId` answers a randomised id. See spec D95. |
| D96 | The forward interfaces of D83 are replaced by the real classes; the overloads of the conductor are one method each; the handlers of a subscription can be `null`; the fakes of the tests are `asConductor` / `asLogBuffers`. See spec D96. |
| D100 | The embedded driver and its clients run on one event loop runner: `MediaDriver.launch` with a mode other than `INVOKER` starts an `AgentRunner` over an `EmbeddedAgent`; a client without `useConductorAgentInvoker` is hosted by it. See spec D100. |
| D101 | The wake up hooks (datagram, send drain, command, `dns.lookup`) and the absence of one in `offer`. See spec D101. |
| D102 | The synchronous calls work because they run the driver inline; a DNS name needs the `async` calls. See spec D102. |
| D103 | `EmbeddedAgent` is a `DynamicCompositeAgent` for one thread. See spec D103. |
| D104 | The network simulation is a fake `dgram` socket under the real transports. See spec D104. |
| D105 | The interop suite is not part of `npm test`: `npm run test:interop` runs it, and it skips every test without the C build. See spec D105. |
| D106 | The C side of the interop tests is two small C programs, `interop_publisher.c` and `interop_subscriber.c`, built by `build-c.mjs` against `libaeron.so`. See spec D106. |
| D107 | The loss of the interop tests is the `loss` interceptor of the C driver for incoming frames, and a seeded loopback relay for the frames a TS subscriber receives. See spec D107. |
| D108 | The interop rows of 8.3 beyond unicast (MDC, multicast, MDS, loss, rejoin, sizes and term rotation) are covered by phase 3. See spec D108. |
| D109 | A late joining subscriber reads from the join position of its image, and a message of 1 to 3 bytes is checked modulo 256 or by length. See spec D109. |
| D110 | The bench harness additions of the gate: `RunBench` `setup` / `teardown` / `throughput`, `bench:gate --file` and `--inject`, and the injected delay of `bench/lib/inject.ts`. See spec D110. |
| D111 | The end-to-end benches: two drivers and two clients in one process, polled by the bench, `MediaDriver.wake()` after an offer, the `dgram` floor apart. See spec D111. |
| D112 | `bench:e2e`: allocation by the sampling heap profiler, the first message after idle, and what CI posts. See spec D112. |
| D113 | `UdpChannelTransport.dispatch` sends with `send(view, callback)` / `send(view, port, address, callback)` over a cache of views of the send slot by length, so that node neither slices nor resolves a host for each datagram. See spec D113. |
| D114 | `AgentRunner` runs up to 16 duty cycles per `setImmediate` while the agent reports work. See spec D114. |
| D115 | The Aeron system tests of phase 3 are ported one to one with launch, wait and Mockito adaptations. See spec D115. |
| D116 | The interop C programs take `-D <destination>` (manual MDC) and grow, rather than switching to the C samples. See spec D116. |
| D117 | The network simulation has multicast groups (membership, TTL, loopback, per-copy loss draws). See spec D117. |
| D118 | The multicast flow control strategies keep their receivers in a growable array and a count, receiver ids and group tags as `(high, low)` halves; the `PreferredMulticastFlowControl` statics are in `PreferredMulticastFlowControlAsf.ts`; `DriverLog` is not called; `FlowControlRegistry` stands for `Class.forName` of the strategies and suppliers; `ReceiverWindow` keeps `FlowControl` off `Configuration`. See spec D118. |
| D119 | A multicast `UdpChannelTransport` (IPv4 and IPv6; a link-local IPv6 group with the interface scope) binds its receive socket to the group address and the port instead of the wildcard address (Java and the C driver turn `IP_MULTICAST_ALL` off, node cannot). The socket then receives no unicast sent to its port, which Java's does. See spec D119. |
| D120 | `CloseHelper.closeAll(errorHandler, closeables)` takes a first argument with `onError` as the error handler even when it has `close` too (`CountedErrorHandler`), if closeables follow it. See spec D120. |
| D121 | The fan-out benches of phase 3 measure one way, offer to the last subscriber, and a window of 100 messages for the stream. See spec D121. |
| D125 | `MediaDriver.Context.resolverInterface` makes `conclude()` throw `ConfigurationException`, as driver name resolution is out of scope. See spec D125. |
| D200 | A registration id is a `number` in the code and a `bigint` in the log metadata; the one conversion is `BigInt(...)` in `DriverConductor.initLogMetadata`, once per log, off the message path. See spec D200. |

A V8 detail that follows from D5: a number outside the 32-bit small integers (a long above 2^31, a double) is boxed in a heap number when it is passed to or returned from a call that is not inlined. `test/extra/agrona/HotPathAllocationTest.test.ts` asserts that the int-sized paths allocate nothing and that the long paths allocate no more than those heap numbers.
